# Business Statistics: question papers in full

19 unique papers · 2018-19 to 2025-26 · Common (all branches), Electronics and Telecommunication, Computer Engineering, Data Science, Artificial Intelligence, Information Technology

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

month will be highly successful?

a. A manufacturing company regularly consumes a special type of glue purchased from a
foreign supplier. Because the supplier is foreign, the time gap between placing an order
and receiving the shipment against that order is long and uncertain. This time gap is called
“lead time.” From past experience, the materials manager notes that the company’s
demand for glue during the uncertain lead time is normally distributed with a mean of
187.6 gallons and a standard deviation of 12.4 gallons. The company follows a policy of
as placing an order when the glue stock falls to a predetermined value called the “reorder
C01, CO2, point.” Note that if the reorder point is x gallons and the demand during lead time exceeds
co3 x gallons, the glue would go “stock-out” and the production process would have to stop. | [5]
BL- Apply , Stock out conditions are therefore serious.
Analyse |. If the reorder point is kept at 187.6 gallons (equal to the mean demand during lead time)
what is the probability that a stock-out condition would occur?
ll. If the reorder point is kept at 200 gallons, what is the probability that a stock-out
condition would occur?
Ill. If the company wants to be 95% confident that the stock-out condition will not occur,
what should be the reorder point? The reorder point minus the mean demand during lead
time is known as the “safety stock.” What is the safety stock in this case?
b. A machine is developed for instantaneous testing of Coronavirus. 4% patients with flu
symptoms actually suffer from Coronavirus. If the disease is actually present, the
a4 probability of positive result is 0.9. If the disease is not present, the probability of
pane positive result is 0.03. . (s]
Analyse, , i) What is the probability that a person detected to suffer from Coronavirus by machine
actually suffers from Coronavirus?
ii) What is the probability that a person detected to be free from Coronavirus by machine
actually suffers from Coronavirus?

SVKM’S NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCHOOL OF TECHNOLOGY MANAGEMENT ENGINEERING

Program MBA.Tech (All Stream) Year: Ui. “Semester: VI

7
| Subject: Business Statistics Marks: 70 -

/
Date : 13 April 2019 « Time:10.00 am to 12.00 noon ~

Duration: 2 (hrs)

No. of Pages:_!4)

Hinal Examination (2018- 1).

Instructions: Candidates should read carefully the instructions sieiaecd on the question
of the Answer Book, which is provided for their use.

1) Question No. _1___ is compulsory.

2) Out of remaining questions, attempt any__4 _ questions.

3) In all___5_ questions to be attempted.

4) All questions carry equal marks.

5) Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.

6) Figures in brackets on the right hand side indicate full marks.
7) Assume Suitable data if necessary.

Question la
The average price of cell phones manufactured by ABCD Ltd. is $98 with a standard deviation of $12. Furthermore,

it is known that the prices of the cell phones manufactured by ABCD Ltd. are normally distributed.

i What percentage of cell phones produced by ABCD Ltd. will have prices of at least $120.20? (2 marks)

ii Cell phones with prices of at least $81.80 will get a free gift. What percentage’ of the cell phones will be eligible
for the free gift? (2 marks)

iii What are the minimum and the maximum values of the middle 95% of cell phone prices? (2 marks)

iv If 7,218 of the cell phone manufactured by ABCD Ltd. cell phones were priced at least $119.00, how many cell
phones were produced by ABCD Ltd.? (2 marks)

Question 1b

The average number of calls received by a call center in a 30-minute period is 15.

ii | What is the probability that between 10:00 and 10:30 the call center will receive more than 9 calls but fewer than
15 calls? (3 marks)

iii What is the probability that between 10:00 and 10:30 the call center will receive fewer than 7 calls? (3 marks)

uestion 2a
an following table shows part of the probability distribution for the number of boats sold daily at Boats Unlimited.
It is known that the average number of boats sold daily is 1.57.
x 0 1 2 3 4 |
f(x) 0.2 0.3 0.32 2 0.05 0.02
Compute the variance and the standard deviation for this probability distribution. (4 marks)

fy
--- page 2 ---
Question 2b

A manufacturing process produces items whose weights are normally distributed. It is known that 22.57% of all the
items produced weigh between 100 grams up to the mean and 49.18% weigh from the mean up to 190 grams.
Determine the mean and the standard deviation. (4 marks)

Question 2c
A survey of a sample of business students resulted in the following information regarding the genders of the
individuals and their selected major.

Selected Major
Gender Finance Marketing Others Total
Male 40 10 30 80
Female ; 30 20 . 70 120
Total 70 30 100 200

i What is the probability of selecting an individual who is majoring in Marketing? (2 marks)

ii What is the probability of selecting an individual who is majoring in Finance, given that the person is
female? (2 marks)

iii Given that a person is male, what is the probability that he is majoring in Finance? (2 marks)

Question 3a

A corporation has 15,000 employees. Sixty-two percent of the employees are male. Twenty-three percent of the

employees earn more than Rs 30,000 a month. Eighteen percent of the employees are male and earn more than Rs

30,000 a month.

i _Ifan employee is taken at random, what is the probability that the employee is male or earns more than Rs
30,000 a month? (2 marks)

ii The employee taken at random turns out to be male. Compute the probability that he earns more than Rs 30,000
a month. (2 marks) ;

iii Are being male and earning more than Rs 30,000 a month independent? (2 marks)

Question 3b

Ina recent survey of computer ownership, 73.4% of the respondents indicated they own PC computers, while 21.8%
indicated they own both PC and Mac computers, and 80.1% said they own at least one of the two computers.

i. What is thé probability that a respondent owns a Mac computer? (2 marks)

ji Given that a respondent owns a PC, what is the probability that the respondent also owns a Mac? (2 marks)

iii Are events “P” and “M” mutually exclusive? Why or why not? Explain using probabilities. (2 marks)

iv Are the two events “P” and “M” independent? Explain, using probabilities. (2 marks)

Question 4a

In one of the management institute, 65% of the employees are male. A random sample of five employees is selected.
Answer the following questions.

i What is the probability that the sample contains exactly three male employees? (2 marks)

ii | What is the probability that the sample contains no male employees? (2 marks)

iii What is the probability that the sample contains more than three female employees? (2 marks)

iv What is the expected number of female employees in the sample? (2 marks)

2}\4
--- page 3 ---
Question 4b
A sample of 9 mothers was taken. The mothers were asked the age of their oldest child. You are given their responses

below.
3 12 4 7 14 6 2 9 11

i Compute the standard deviation. (2 marks)
ii Compute the coefficient of variation. (2 marks)
iii Determine the 25th percentile. (2 marks)

Question 5a

MNM Corporation gives each of its employees an aptitude test. The scores on the test are normally distributed with a
mean of 75 and a standard deviation of 15. A simple random sample of 25 is taken from a population of 500.

i What are the expected value, the standard deviation, and the shape of the sampling distribution of x? (2 marks)
ii What is the probability that the average aptitude test in the sample will be between 70.14 and 82.14? (2 marks)
iii What is the probability that the average aptitude test in the sample will be greater than 82.68? (2 marks)

iV Finda value, C, such that P(x = C) = .015. (2 marks)

Question 5b

Average U.S. daily internet use at home is two hours and twenty minutes. A sample of 64 homes in Soddy-Daisy

showed an average usage of two hours and fifty minutes with a standard deviation of eighty minutes. We are

interested in determining whether or not the average usage in Soddy-Daisy is significantly different from the U.S.

average.

i State the null and alternative hypotheses to be tested. (2 marks) TT

ii | Compute the test statistic. (2 marks)

iii The null hypothesis is to be tested at 95% confidence. Determine the critical value(s) for this test. What do you
conclude? (2 marks) :

Question 6a
The results of a recent study regarding smoking and three types of illness are shown in the following table.

Illness Non-Smokers Smokers Totals
Emphysema 50 150 200
Heart problem 50 150 200
Cancer 100 500 600
Totals 200 800 1000

We are interested in determining whether or not illness is independent of smoking.

i State the null and alternative hypotheses to be tested. (2 marks)

ii Show the contingency table of the expected frequencies and determine the test statistic. (2 marks)

iii The null hypothesis is to be tested at 95% confidence. Determine the critical value for this test. What do you
conclude? (2 marks)

Question 6b
You are given the following information obtained from a random sample of 6 observations. Assume the population
has a normal distribution.

14 20 21 16 18 19

3)14
--- page 4 ---
i What is the point estimate of 1? (2 marks)

ii Construct an 80% confidence interval for 1. (2 marks)

iii Construct a 98% confidence interval for p1. (2 marks)

iv Discuss why the 80% and 98% confidence intervals are different. (2 marks)

Question 7a
We are interested in determining whether or not the variances of the starting salaries of accounting majors is
significantly different from management majors. The following information was gathered from two samples.

‘ Accounting Management
Sample Size 21 18
Average Monthly Income $3,600 $3,500
Standard Deviation “ $900 $400

At 90% confidence, test to determine whether or not the variances are equal. (4 marks)

Question 7b

The average weekly earnings of bus drivers in a city are $950 (that is j1) with a standard deviation of $45 (that is 0).

Assume that we select a random sample of 81 bus drivers.

i What is the probability that the sample mean will be greater than $960? (2 marks)

ii If the population of bus drivers consisted of 400 drivers, what would be the standard error of the mean? (2
marks)

Question 7c

It is said that more males register to vote in a national election than females. A research organization selected a

random sample of 300 registered voters and reported that 165 of the registered voters were male.

i Formulate the hypotheses for this problem. (2 marks)

ii | Compute the test statistic. (2 marks)

iii Using the p-value approach, can you conclude that more males registered to vote than females? Let a = .05.
(2 marks)

wes Kms

Q.1a) The following data represent the number of passengers per flight in a sample of 50 flights,
23, 46, 66, 67, 13, 58, 19, 17, 65, 17, 25, 20, 47, 28, 16, 38, 44, 29, 48, 29, 69, 34, 35, 60, 37, 52, 80,
59, 51, 33, 48, 46, 23, 38, 52, 50, 17, 57, 41,77, 45, 47, 49, 19, 32, 64, 27, 61, 70, 19

a) Construct a frequency distribution table with eight equal intervals. (8)

b) Draw a histogram of distribution :

c) Find the mean of data.

b) Suppose the following data are selected randomly from a population of normally distributed
values. 40, 51, 43, 48, 44,57, 54, 39, 42, 48, 45, 39, 43
Construct a 95% confidence interval to estimate the population mean. (6)

Q.2 a) The mean of 5 observations is 15 and the variance is 9. If two more observations having
values -3 and 10 are combined with these 5 observations, what will be the new mean and variance

of 7 observations? (7)

b) Machines A, B, and C all produce the same two parts, X and Y, Of all the parts produced, machine
A, Band C produces 60%, 30%, and 10% respectively. In addition, 40% of the parts made by
machine Aare part X. 50% of the parts made by machine B are part X. 70% of the parts made by
machine C are part X. A part produced by this company is randomly sampled and is determined to
be an X part. With the knowledge that it is an X part, Find the probabilities that the part came from

machine A. (7)
Vis
--- page 2 ---
‘, Q.3 a) Given that the switch board of consultant's office receives on an average 0.9 calls per

minute, which is Poisson distributed. Find the probability (i) In a given minute there will be at
least one incoming call (ii) Between 10.00AM and 10.02 AM there-will be exactly 2 incoming calls
(iii) During an interval of 4 minutes there will be at most 3 incoming calls.

(7)

b) A population is normally distributed, with a mean of 23.45 anda standard deviation of 3.8.
What is the probability of each of the following?

(7)

I. Taking a sample of size 10 and obtaining a sample mean of 22 or more
Il. Taking a sample of size 4 and getting a sample mean of more than 26

Q.4.a) Arandom sample of 50 households was selected for a telephone survey. The key question
asked was, “Do you or any member of your household own a cellular telephone with a built-in
camera?” Of the 50 respondents, 15 said yes and 35 said no.

(7)

i. Determine the sample proportion; p, of households with cellular telephones with built-in
cameras. :

ii. If the population proportion is 0.40, determine the standard error of the proportion.

iii. For what value of sample proportion p , 80% of sample proportion is less than p?

b) Arandom sample size 65 was taken to estimate the mean annual income of 1,000 families and
mean and standard deviation were found to be % 6,300 and % 9.50 respectively. Find a 95%
confidence interval for the population mean. (7)

Q.5 a) A sample poll of 100 voters chosen at random from all voters ina given district indicated
that 55% of them were in favour of a particular candidate. Find (i) 95% and (ii) 99% confidence
limits for the proportion of all the voters in favour of this candidate.

(7)

b) A fertilizer mixing machine is set to give 12 kg of nitrate for every quintal bag of fertilizer. Ten
100 kg bags are examined. The percentage of nitrate is as follows: 11, 14, 13, 12, 13,12, 13, 14,11
and 12. Is there reason to believe that the machine is defective at 5% significance label?

(7)

Q.6 a) A certain drug is claimed to be effective in curing cold. In an experiment on 500 persons
with cold, half of them were given the drug and half of them were given the sugar pills. The
patient's reactions to the treatment are recorded in the following table:

(7)

Helped | Harmed | No effect | Total
Drug 150 30 70 250
Ars
--- page 3 ---
Sugar pills | 130 40 80 250

Total 280 70 150 500

On the basis of the data can it be concluded that there is a significant difference (at 0.05 significant
label) in the effect of the drug and sugar pills?

b) Two random sample gave the following results: (7)

Sample | size | Sample Mean | Sum of squares of Deviations From Mean
1 10 | 15 90
2 12 | 14 108

Assuming normal population, test for the equality of population variances at the 5% level of
significance.

Q.7 a) A sample of 87 professional working women showed that the average amount paid annually
into a private pension fund per person was $3352. The population standard deviation is $1100. A
sample of 76 professional working men showed that the average amount paid annually into a
private pension fund per person was $5727, with a population standard deviation of $1700. A
women’s activist group wants to “prove” that women do not pay as much per year as men into
private pension funds. If they use a@,= .001 and these sample data, will they be able to reject a null
hypothesis that women annually pay the same as or more than men into private pension funds?

(7)

b) An IQ test was administered to 5 persons before and after they were trained.-The result is given
below: (7)
Candidates 1 [u [m [wv [v

1Q before training | 110 | 120 | 123 | 132 | 125
IQ aftertraining | 120 | 118 | 125 | 136 | 121
Test whether there is any change in IQ after the training programme.
